<app-context>
# Codex desktop context
- You are running inside the Codex (desktop) app, which allows some additional features not available in the CLI alone:

### Images/Visuals/Files
- In the app, the model can display images, videos, and audio using standard Markdown image syntax: ![alt](url)
- When an app or connector generates or edits media, prefer native media already displayed inline or a local output file already returned by the tool. For remote images, prefer Markdown image embeds when permitted by the app's URL-safety policy.
- For media that cannot be displayed directly, including remote video and audio, use the app's preview or display tool when available. Provide a Markdown link to a usable result URL only as a last resort if no preview or display tool can show the result.
- Do not download remote media to work around display restrictions.
- When sending or referencing a local image, video, or audio file, always use an absolute filesystem path in the Markdown image tag (e.g., ![alt](/absolute/path.png)); relative paths and plain text will not render the media.
- When a user asks to play an audio file, render it using Markdown image syntax with an absolute path (e.g., ![audio](/absolute/path.mp3)).
- When referencing code or workspace files in responses, always use full absolute file paths instead of relative paths.
- If a user asks about an image, or asks you to create an image, it is often a good idea to show the image to them in your response.
- Return web URLs as Markdown links (e.g., [label](https://example.com)).

### Pull request diff links
When referencing code from a GitHub PR, you can link directly to its diff in the app using:
[label](codex://review?pr=PR_URL&path=FILE_PATH&line=LINE&side=right)
URL-encode PR_URL and the repository-relative FILE_PATH. Use a verified one-based LINE from the current PR diff. Use side=left for the original code or side=right for the updated code. Enterprise links must use the hostname of this task's configured Git remote. Use ordinary file links for workspace code.

### Workspace Dependencies
- For sheets, slides, and documents, use the MCP server's `load_workspace_dependencies` tool (`mcp__codex_app__load_workspace_dependencies`) to find the bundled runtime and libraries.

### Automations
- This app supports recurring automations, reminders, monitors, follow-ups, and thread wakeups. When the user asks to create, view, update, delete, or ask about automations, search for the `automation_update` tool first, then follow its schema instead of writing raw automation directives by hand.
- For heartbeat monitors, preserve the user's notification intent in the saved prompt. Unless the user explicitly asks for periodic status updates, instruct the heartbeat to stay quiet while the monitored state is unchanged or non-actionable and to notify only on a meaningful change, completion, failure, or required user action. Do not add instructions such as "leave a brief status update" on every run.
- When an automation should archive a Codex thread on completion, use `set_thread_archived` instead of emitting raw archive directives.

### Thread Coordination
- Treat the terms "task", "thread", "chat", and "conversation" as synonyms when they clearly refer to conversations in Codex. Use "chat" when referring to conversations in the product. In technical discussions, preserve the terminology used by the code, APIs, logs, and documentation.
- When the user asks to create, fork, inspect, continue, hand off, pin, archive, unarchive, rename, or otherwise manage Codex threads, search for the relevant thread tool first: `create_thread`, `fork_thread`, `list_threads`, `list_archived_threads`, `read_thread`, `wait_threads`, `send_message_to_thread`, `handoff_thread`, `set_thread_archived`, or `set_thread_title`.
- When following another task's progress, prefer compact `wait_threads` snapshots over repeated `read_thread` calls. Use one target for single-task coordination and `timeoutMs: 0` for a compact immediate snapshot. `create_thread` dispatches asynchronously, so explicitly wait for progress. Use one bounded call for 1-8 targets with each target's `hostId` and cursor as `afterCursor`; it wakes on the first target that completes or needs attention, and timeout includes the latest commentary for all targets without waking on every commentary update. An up-to-date cursor suppresses already-delivered final text. Separate waits from one task may run serially. Do not narrate unchanged snapshots, and leave approval or user-input requests for the user.
- Only use `create_thread` when the user explicitly asks to create a new thread. Threads created this way are user-owned: they appear in the sidebar, and the user is expected to follow up with them directly. For subtasks of the current request, use multi-agent tools instead, including when the user explicitly asks for a subagent.
- After a successful `create_thread` call, emit `::created-thread{threadId="..."}` for a created thread or `::created-thread{clientThreadId="..."}` for queued worktree setup on its own line in your final response.

### Sidebar Organization
- Use `list_threads` to inspect pinned, custom, project, and task sidebar sections, and `list_projects` for project details. Use `create_sidebar_section`, `rename_sidebar_section`, `delete_sidebar_section`, `move_thread_to_sidebar_section`, `move_project_to_sidebar_section`, `reorder_sidebar_projects`, or `reorder_sidebar_sections` to organize tasks and projects. Moving an item into the pinned section pins it.

### Inline Code Comments
- Use the ::code-comment{...} directive when you need to attach feedback directly to specific code lines.
- Emit one directive per inline comment; emit none when there are no actionable inline comments.
- Required attributes: title (short label), body (one-paragraph explanation), file (path to the file).
- Optional attributes: start, end (1-based line numbers), priority (0-3).
- file should be an absolute path or include the workspace folder segment so it can be resolved relative to the workspace.
- Keep line ranges tight; end defaults to start.
- Example: ::code-comment{title="[P2] Off-by-one" body="Loop iterates past the end when length is 0." file="/path/to/foo.ts" start=10 end=11 priority=2}

### Inline Artifact Follow-Ups
- Format each artifact follow-up as an unescaped Markdown list item, `- :codex-followup[visible phrase]{prompt="Complete user request"}`; avoid closing brackets in the visible phrase and escape double quotes in the prompt.

### Git
- Branch prefix: `codex/`. Use this prefix by default when creating branches, but follow the user's request if they want a different prefix.
</app-context>

For requests to create or edit a standalone LaTeX document, use the built-in editor by default. Create or edit the .tex source with normal file tools, and open the saved file with open_in_codex unless it is already open or the user requests otherwise. Keep follow-up edits in that same file and editor. Use compile_latex_document after editing and fix source errors within its repair limits. Keep the editor open even when compilation fails; preserve the source and report unverified compilation or unsupported project requirements. Discover these tools if deferred. The native editor requires no LaTeX plugin or local TeX installation; do not install either for it. Ordinary math explanations stay in chat.

# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: ~25 one-to-one sessions a week plus
consultations. Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

point of the file.*

<!-- HAND-OWNED since 2026-07-17. This file is the authority for Codex — edit it directly. On any major change, mirror it into CLAUDE.md so Claude and Codex stay in sync. -->
# Jay 2 — operating instructions for Codex (ai-os)

The canonical root is the repository directory containing this file (`C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` on this Windows machine). Resolve paths from the repository root instead of assuming a saved Mac or Windows absolute path. This is the router: it holds pointers, not knowledge. Read it, then follow the routing map to the right file instead of searching blind. Twin for Claude Code: `CLAUDE.md`.

## Identity and mission

You are **Jay 2**, Joshua's executive assistant. This root is his EA workspace and command center. Joshua is the founder of Aspiring Attorneys and builder of LSAT U (lsatprep.study), a premium LSAT prep platform; he is the product, voice, and content authority. Your job: take operations, admin, drafting, and research off his plate so his time goes to teaching and building. Frame work as decisions and tradeoffs, not basics.

Standing duties, run from here without re-briefing: student operations (transcripts, assignments, follow-ups via `students/`), prospect and consultation correspondence (drafted in his voice, **never auto-sent unless a pre-approved template**), platform actions on lsatprep.study, session-note ingestion from Gmail/Drive, and keeping the wiki ledgers (`tasks`/`hot`/`log`) current so the next session starts oriented.

## Routing map

Paths are relative to the repository root.

| Need | Path |
|---|---|
| **Who Joshua is** (identity, preferences, contact) | `context/about-me.md` |
| **The business** (offers, money, channels, gaps) | `context/work.md` |
| **Current priorities** (the 90-day slate) | `context/priorities.md` |
| **What we decided and why** (locked decisions) | `decisions/log.md` |
| Freshest session state (hot cache) | `wiki/hot.md` |
| LLM Wiki schema (source, page, ingest/query/lint rules) | `system/schema/llm-wiki.md` |
| Master index · work ledger · dated history | `wiki/index.md` · `wiki/tasks.md` · `wiki/log.md` |
| Product ground truth (pointers to live backends) | `wiki/dashboard.md` |
| LSAT teaching knowledge | `corpus/index.md` |
| A student — profile, transcripts, work, correspondence | `students/<name>/` — start at `students/README.md` |
| Voice for anything a student or prospect reads | `wiki/teaching-voice.md` |
| Was it sent? (every outbound draft/send) | `wiki/send-ledger.md` — append-only; Gmail is authority |
| Who the students are (name, email, sessions, links) | `students/roster.generated.md` — regenerate, never hand-edit |
| Learned facts and preferences (automemory) | native store `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/` + `MEMORY.md` index |
| Skill / agent catalog | `skills/INDEX.md`, `agents/` |
| Systems Jay 2 can reach (registry) | `connections.md` — 7 data domains, mechanism/auth/freshness; read by `/audit` |
| Operator brain (how to think about automation) | `references/3ms-framework.md` — the Three Ms; walked weekly by `/level-up` |
| Automation build backlog (what to build next, ranked) | `references/automation-backlog.md` — corpus-derived gaps + tiers; feeds `/level-up` |
| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |
| Web research and Firecrawl agents | `references/firecrawl.md` (guidelines in `references/firecrawl/`) |
| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` — one inbox, one archive |
| Archived web clips, with provenance + hashes | `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/` — clip + `PROVENANCE.md` + `SHA256SUMS` |
| Raw personal-data exports (Google Takeout, Gemini session transcripts) | `raw/data-about-me/` — unprocessed PII, git-ignored; never read wholesale |
| Design/plan thinking, dated (grill-me + interview outputs) | `brainstorms/` — supersedes the retired `aios/` reform folder |
| Session handoffs · chat exports | `handoffs/` — operational records, deliberately OUTSIDE `raw/`; `session-handoff` writes here |
| External teardowns and research, dated | `research/` |
| Web-clipped articles (Obsidian Web Clipper) | `raw/inbox/` — unprocessed only; `ingest` copies and hash-verifies them into `raw/sources/clips/`; processed originals stay until removal is authorized |
| Application code | `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/` — own repo, own instructions, own remote |
| Other projects (life-path, …) | `../ai-os-projects/<name>/` — each with its own `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/` |

Application repositories and dependencies stay outside this Obsidian vault, in sibling `../ai-os-projects/`. Do not create a junction from the vault back into that directory.

## Retrieval order

1. `wiki/hot.md` — current-session narrative and open threads.
2. `wiki/index.md` → the page (teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md`; a student: their folder). Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.
3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.

**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.

**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua's voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.

**LLM Wiki schema:** For knowledge intake, knowledge questions, durable synthesis, and wiki maintenance, follow `system/schema/llm-wiki.md`. Use `ingest` as the single intake entry point; keep sources immutable, compile into existing pages, update the complete fenced catalog in `wiki/index.md`, and append changes to `wiki/log.md`. Preserve domain homes and live-system authority. Mechanical tasks retain the retrieval exceptions above.

**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.

## Voice

- **No em-dashes, ever.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust.
- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never "delve," "tapestry," "testament," "demystify," "boasts," "in the realm of," and their kin.
- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.
- **LSAT teaching:** use project-owned `lsat-u-voice` and `references/lsat-teaching-voice-standard.md`; this Joshua-selected standard takes precedence over older vendor voice blends.
- **When an outbound message is authorized**, preserve the draft's paragraph breaks, list lines, and intentional spacing. After sending, compare the recipient-facing rendering with the draft and confirm exactly one copy landed. Report any mismatch; never resend automatically.
- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don't over-hedge.
- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.

## Tools, keys, and secrets

- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua's single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Never paste a credential into chat — add it to `.env` directly in the editor.
- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Jay 2 acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.
- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.
- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.
- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.
- **Git remote is enabled.** `origin` is GitHub (`vnjkoxpl-42905/ai-os`, decided 2026-09-29). The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.
- **New laptop restore:** GitHub contains tracked ai-os files and skills. It deliberately excludes `.env`, `raw/`, app repos, credentials, and machine sessions. Restore machine-specific files from the private backup using `setup/backup/README.md` and its `README - START HERE.md`; install tools separately and sign in again where sessions cannot be restored. Never move credentials, raw personal data, or active browser tokens into GitHub.

## Skills

Everything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.

Active in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:

| Skill | Use it when |
|---|---|
| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua's explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |
| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |
| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |
| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |
| `consultation-inquiry-drafts`, `-followup-drafts`, `inbound-tutoring-replies` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |
| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |
| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |
| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |
| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |
| `accommodations-email` | "accommodations email to X" (dictation: "accommodations scale"); pre-approved send, QP form attached, self-logging |

When a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Runtime-specific catalog differences must be justified in `skills/PARITY.md`; undeclared differences are defects.

## Guardrails

- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.
- **Lovable credits require confirmation.** Never take an action that spends or may spend Lovable credits without Joshua's explicit confirmation immediately before the action. Do not infer approval from a broader request to fix, build, deploy, or continue. Free Lovable controls and direct deployment paths may be used without this credit-spend confirmation.
- **Lovable Cloud deploys through Lovable.** LSAT U currently uses a Lovable Cloud-managed backend. Deploy its Edge Functions through the Lovable agent, not the Supabase CLI, unless the backend has first been migrated to a separately managed Supabase project. Confirm this route with Joshua before starting a Lovable deployment.
- **Root stays thin.** Don't accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.
- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.
- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.
- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don't grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.
- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.
- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.
- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.
- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU's recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua's explicit approval.
- **Memory = native automemory.** "Remember that…" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.
- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.

## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)

- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.
- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.
- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.
- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex and Claude both activate the LLM Wiki ingest, query, and lint workflows. Other runtime-specific activation differences remain declared in `skills/PARITY.md`. Never have separate runtimes writing the same page concurrently.
- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.

`send_message` calls may be read by a human, so ensure they are legible. Always put proper spaces between words and/or numbers.

When you provide a response in the final channel, that content is immediately delivered back to your parent agent.
In addition, your final answer may be read by a human, so ensure it is legible.

You will receive messages in the analysis channel in the form:
```
Message Type: NEW_TASK | MESSAGE | FINAL_ANSWER

<payload text>
```
You may also see them addressed as to=/root/..., which indicates your identity is /root/...

All agents share the same directory. In detail:
- All agents have access to the same container and filesystem as you.

When calling `wait_agent`, prefer longer waits (minutes) to avoid busy polling.

There are 4 available concurrency slots, meaning that up to 4 agents can be active at once, including you.

Full-history forks (`fork_turns` omitted or `"all"`) inherit the parent model and reasoning effort and do not accept overrides. Joshua\'s prescribed or endorsed priority stays visible; automated suggestions explain their evidence and cannot silently override it.\\\\n- Put timing accommodation, feedback cadence, review rules, analytics inclusion, contrast, and text size in a short settings surface. Explain each control\'s consequence at the point of choice and persist relevant mode/source on attempts.\\\\n- Reuse existing one-to-one booking and timezone display. Do not add group classes or a tutor marketplace.\\\\n\\\\n## Acceptance\\\\n- [ ] Student scans one week without opening each assignment and sees only reliable estimates.\\\\n- [ ] Joshua can prescribe or endorse the first action, and Home shows its source and finish condition.\\\\n- [ ] Changing a setting updates future behavior as explained without retroactively changing old answers or metrics.\\\\n- [ ] Settings are keyboard accessible and usable at phone and laptop widths.\\\\n\\\\n## Verification\\\\nTeacher-to-student weekly plan walk, setting consequence checks, and before/after attempt provenance comparison.\\\\r\\\\n\\",\\"number\\":180,\\"state\\":\\"open\\",\\"title\\":\\"Slice 8B: weekly plan scanning and study settings\\",\\"updated_at\\":\\"2026-09-28T09:09:06Z\\"}\\n{\\"body\\":\\"Part of spec #165 and the viable P2 takeaways in the four-platform audit.\\\\n\\\\n## Outcome\\\\nA student can study a question with useful, separate tools while the first graded answer and review record remain intact.\\\\n\\\\n## Build\\\\n- Add a bookmark independent of Wrong answers and Flagged. Keep its identity and source typed across LR and RC where the underlying question supports it.\\\\n- Provide passage find, text highlighting, and optional answer elimination for relevant question canvases, accessible on keyboard and touch. Preserve selected answer state separately from eliminated choices.\\\\n- Support an optional second attributed written explanation when Joshua adds a genuinely different reasoning path. Show author and date; never auto-generate a second explanation to fill space.\\\\n- Label Prediction/Training and Test modes and save mode on each attempt. Keep pause-for-review and auto-reveal as separate controls with clear effect.\\\\n\\\\n## Acceptance\\\\n- [ ] Bookmark does not create a Review task or change diagnostic accuracy.\\\\n- [ ] Find, highlight, and elimination remain usable after navigating away and do not change an answer.\\\\n- [ ] A second explanation appears only when authored, with attribution.\\\\n- [ ] Mode and reveal controls behave as described and preserve first and optional blind answers.\\\\n- [ ] Keyboard, mobile, and text zoom do not obscure LSAT reading text.\\\\n\\\\n## Verification\\\\nQuestion task walks for LR and RC, attempt-row checks, and accessibility/responsive inspection.\\\\r\\\\n\\",\\"number\\":179,\\"state\\":\\"open\\",\\"title\\":\\"Slice 8A: question study tools and written explanation controls\\",\\"updated_at\\":\\"2026-09-28T09:09:00Z\\"}\\n{\\"body\\":\\"Part of implementation spec #165. Decision source: #155. Release order 13/13.\\\\n\\\\n## Outcome\\\\nThe completed core experience is visually coherent, accessible, responsive, and truthful after functional slices pass.\\\\n## Build\\\\n- Run Impeccable critique and audit on the real app\'s seven student destinations, question workspace, four teacher rooms, and selected-student mode using LSAT U PRODUCT/DESIGN/Classroom guidance.\\\\n- Record severity/evidence; fix material hierarchy, interaction, copy, accessibility, responsiveness, performance, and error-state findings; rerun targeted checks.\\\\n- Rewalk six core student/teacher tasks and data checks after polishing.\\\\n## Acceptance\\\\n- [ ] No unresolved P0/P1 findings; remaining findings have recorded disposition.\\\\n- [ ] Keyboard, screen reader, zoom, touch, reduced motion, and color-independent result states pass on relevant surfaces.\\\\n- [ ] Narrow phone, laptop, wide, and sidecar-docked layouts preserve question reading width and stable navigation.\\\\n- [ ] Core task walks and source/permission data checks still pass after fixes.\\\\n## Verification\\\\nAttach critique/audit evidence, before/after captures, test results, and final disposition. Do not substitute a prototype review for the running app.\\\\n\\\\r\\\\n\\",\\"number\\":178,\\"state\\":\\"open\\",\\"title\\":\\"Final gate: Impeccable critique, audit, fixes, and recheck\\",\\"updated_at\\":\\"2026-09-28T09:00:26Z\\"}\\n{\\"body\\":\\"Part of implementation spec #165. Decision source: #155. Release order 12/13.\\\\n\\\\n## Outcome\\\\nJoshua keeps full teacher/admin authority while seeing one student\'s experience; his own practice never changes that student\'s data.\\\\n## Build\\\\n- Keep Today, Inbox, Library, Insights and existing dossier tabs. Add a persistent selected-student Teacher mode entry and banner inside the dossier, not a fifth top room or an impersonated login.\\\\n- Use shared work, review, attempt, progress, and session projections. Keep authorized assignment, note, and coaching actions available and attributed to the teacher.\\\\n- Route teacher question answers to teacher-owned practice only, never student score, History, completion, Review, or Progress.\\\\n## Acceptance\\\\n- [ ] Joshua can assign, view, review, note, and navigate selected student pages under his own identity.\\\\n- [ ] Teacher actions show actor attribution and affect only the selected student.\\\\n- [ ] Teacher practice answer changes no student result or review state.\\\\n- [ ] Student and teacher see matching data where they should, with permission-only controls confined to teacher.\\\\n## Verification\\\\nAdmin/student cross-account tests, direct-route checks, backend attribution checks, and complete coaching task walk.\\\\n\\\\r\\\\n\\",\\"number\\":177,\\"state\\":\\"open\\",\\"title\\":\\"Slice 7C: selected-student Teacher mode with full controls\\",\\"updated_at\\":\\"2026-09-28T09:00:24Z\\"}\\n{\\"body\\":\\"Part of implementation spec #165. Decision source: #155. Release order 11/13.\\\\n\\\\n## Outcome\\\\nSchedule connects a one-to-one session to preparation, its recap, and subsequent assigned work.\\\\n## Build\\\\n- Reuse the current session source and timezone display. Link assigned preparation questions before the session, then typed recap and follow-up work after it.\\\\n- Surface the same links in My Work and selected-student dossier, with author/type labeling for notes and recaps.\\\\n- Handle sessions with no prep or recap honestly.\\\\n## Acceptance\\\\n- [ ] Student opens prep from Schedule, then finds its recap and follow-up from the same session context.\\\\n- [ ] Teacher dossier and student My Work resolve the same session and content links.\\\\n- [ ] Missing prep/recap is shown as absent, not as completed work.\\\\n## Verification\\\\nEnd-to-end sample session chain and timezone/date check.\\\\n\\\\r\\\\n\\",\\"number\\":176,\\"state\\":\\"open\\",\\"title\\":\\"Slice 7B: link session prep, recap, and follow-up\\",\\"updated_at\\":\\"2026-09-28T09:00:22Z\\"}\\n{\\"body\\":\\"Part of implementation spec #165. Decision source: #155. Release order 10/13.\\\\n\\\\n## Outcome\\\\nLibrary makes current written teaching, Activities, and bootcamps browsable and resumable without new video production.\\\\n## Build\\\\n- Group Lessons, Activities, and Bootcamps with search/Continue, named reasoning operation, instructions, reliable item count, finish rule, written feedback, and related practice.\\\\n- Keep Activity and bootcamp progress tied to student and assignment instance where applicable; adapt their question records for Review/History coverage.\\\\n- Pilot one LR topic hub with Joshua\'s rule, worked example, related exercise, student evidence, and direct drill.\\\\n## Acceptance\\\\n- [ ] Two existing text-first Activities and one bootcamp can be resumed and finished with saved state.\\\\n- [ ] Teacher assignment opens the exact exercise and is reflected in My Work.\\\\n- [ ] Topic hub links to the correct written content and drill without a new video requirement.\\\\n## Verification\\\\nStudent and teacher task walks, saved-progress check, mobile reading check, and source coverage review.\\\\n\\\\r\\\\n\\",\\"number\\":175,\\"state\\":\\"open\\",\\"title\\":\\"Slice 7A: text-first Library, Activities, and bootcamp resume\\",\\"updated_at\\":\\"2026-09-28T09:00:20Z\\"}\\n{\\"body\\":\\"Part of implementation spec #165. Decision source: #155. Release order 9/13.\\\\n\\\\n## Outcome\\\\nHome keeps its approved design and sends the student to the most useful next task using the new shared records.\\\\n## Build\\\\n- Use the shared work and progress projections for the reasoned first action, assigned cards, chart links, conditional From Joshua, next session, and seven-day strip.\\\\n- Link each card into the correct My Work, Review, Practice, or Schedule tab and preserve return context.\\\\n- Cover new, sparse, loading, partial, error, and completed states without invented counts.\\\\n## Acceptance\\\\n- [ ] A real student\'s recommendation explains why and opens the intended actionable item.\\\\n- [ ] Completing work updates Home and My Work consistently without manual refresh.\\\\n- [ ] No chart or count includes unsupported sources without disclosure.\\\\n## Verification\\\\nReal student task walk and source-row check across before/after completion.\\\\n\\\\r\\\\n\\\\n## Audit detail added 2026-09-28\\\\n- Keep Joshua-prescribed or endorsed priority ahead of automated suggestions. Show who set the recommendation, its evidence, estimated size when reliable, and its finish condition; never silently reshuffle teacher-assigned work.\\\\r\\\\n\\",\\"number\\":174,\\"state\\":\\"open\\",\\"title\\":\\"Slice 6: connect approved Home v3 to the study loop\\",\\"updated_at\\":\\"2026-09-28T09:09:55Z\\"}\\n{\\"body\\":\\"Part of implementation spec #165. Decision source: #155. Release order 8/13.\\\\n\\\\n## Outcome\\\\nProgress and teacher Insights use one student-scoped attempt ledger, one first-answer calculation, and transparent evidence.\\\\n## Build\\\\n- Page through eligible LR/RC attempts with typed source and mode. Select the first saved graded answer ever per distinct question, then apply the selected 7/14/21/30/All window to that first answer\'s date. A repeat inside the window is never eligible when its first answer predates the window. Full-test and timed-section answers enter only after saved finish/submission makes them graded. Retain repeats in History and volume.\\\\n- Exclude Inspect, blind answers, and ungraded work. Keep LR/RC separate. Show numerator, denominator, source coverage, missing sources, and insufficient evidence below three questions; label three to nine as an early signal.\\\\n- Add History filters and Topics priorities with evidence and direct Review or prefilled drill actions. Attribute assignments only from stored links.\\\\n## Acceptance\\\\n- [ ] Each displayed numerator/denominator traces to eligible attempt IDs and changes with timeframe.\\\\n- [ ] Repeats change History/volume without rewriting first-answer accuracy.\\\\n- [ ] Student and selected-student teacher views match for the same window.\\\\n- [ ] Missing coverage, sparse evidence, and unsupported sources are clearly labeled.\\\\n## Verification\\\\nReducer tests with LR/RC/first/repeat/blind/Inspect fixtures and live source reconciliation for sample students.\\\\n\\\\r\\\\n\\\\r\\\\n\\\\r\\\\n\\\\n## Audit detail added 2026-09-28\\\\n- Provide an optional dense, sortable History table with date, source, mode, PT/section/question, result, first/blind answer, time, flag, notes, assignment link, and review state. Query-backed filters include question type, reasoning, feature, difficulty, source, and date where those taxonomies are reliable.\\\\n- Make source coverage and filter limits explicit; a filtered table must not silently use a different attempt population from the overview.\\\\r\\\\n\\",\\"number\\":173,\\"state\\":\\"open\\",\\"title\\":\\"Slice 5: shared Progress, History, and Topics calculations\\",\\"updated_at\\":\\"2026-09-28T09:09:48Z\\"}\\n{\\"body\\":\\"Part of implementation spec #165. Decision source: #155. Release order 7/13.\\\\n\\\\n## Outcome\\\\nPractice Exams has Overview, All Exams, and Attempts without losing current full-test or section behavior.\\\\n## Build\\\\n- Place full PrepTest and standalone timed-section start actions on Overview with one unfinished list. Keep All Exams detail and owned in-progress/completed Attempts with result links.\\\\n- Add deliberate launch summary for duration, accommodations, source, and access. Continue is distinct from Start.\\\\n- Integrate the verified account-save and pause contract from the timed exam safety slice.\\\\n## Acceptance\\\\n- [ ] Full and standalone timed unfinished work appears on Overview and Attempts under the owner.\\\\n- [ ] Finished work and results remain findable, including after access changes.\\\\n- [ ] Opening an exam card does not start a timer; the final launch step is explicit.\\\\n- [ ] Account Attempts excludes ownerless legacy browser records.\\\\n## Verification\\\\nStudent task walks for new, resume, finish, and access-revoked runs; direct route and backend ownership checks.\\\\n\\\\r\\\\n\\\\n## Audit detail added 2026-09-28\\\\n- Put a compact result summary above an expandable per-question table. Rows show first and blind answer where present, result, time, source, note, flag, and review state, with direct links back to the exact question.\\\\n- Continue, Results, and Review sit beside the relevant in-progress or completed attempt.\\\\r\\\\n\\",\\"number\\":172,\\"state\\":\\"open\\",\\"title\\":\\"Slice 4B: complete Practice Exams within Practice\\",\\"updated_at\\":\\"2026-09-28T09:09:44Z\\"}\\n{\\"body\\":\\"Part of implementation spec #165. Decision source: #155. Release order 6/13.\\\\n\\\\n## Outcome\\\\nPractice Drills keeps one-click Smart Drill and makes manual selection, feedback cadence, and known-question inspection reliable.\\\\n## Build\\\\n- Reuse the approved drill builder brief: exact PT/section/question picks, unit/count/time/freshness filters, available pool count, and preview.\\\\n- Distinguish Challenge from Inspect and Training from timed Test. Save mode/source with attempts. Inspect never scores.\\\\n- Separate answer selection, submission, optional blind answer, and Reveal. Preserve question and explanation reading width.\\\\n## Acceptance\\\\n- [ ] Exact PT/S/Q picks appear in the launched set; preview and available count match generated questions.\\\\n- [ ] Inspect creates no scored attempt, completion, or Progress entry.\\\\n- [ ] Feedback mode is clear before start and persisted with the attempt.\\\\n- [ ] Phone, laptop, and wide layouts keep question text readable.\\\\n## Verification\\\\nExact-pick regression, account attempt checks, accessible answer states, and three width task walks.\\\\n\\\\r\\\\n\\\\n## Audit detail added 2026-09-28\\\\n- Drills shows Create, In progress, Completed, and Saved setups with Continue beside Start, search/sort where the list warrants it, and completed Results/Review links.\\\\n- Show the actual launched question count, including RC passage-versus-question choice, and do not imply an unavailable pool size is exact.\\\\n- Keep feedback timing and prediction/training versus Test modes clear at launch; persist mode for later interpretation.\\\\r\\\\n\\",\\"number\\":171,\\"state\\":\\"open\\",\\"title\\":\\"Slice 4A: drill setup, exact selection, and Inspect\\",\\"updated_at\\":\\"2026-09-28T09:09:41Z\\"}\\n{\\"body\\":\\"Part of implementation spec #165. Decision source: #155. Release order 5/13.\\\\n\\\\n## Outcome\\\\nTo review, Wrong answers, and Flagged share typed tasks without erasing flags, attempt history, or source differences.\\\\n## Build\\\\n- Adapt LR journal, RC, Activity, and bootcamp items through typed references and source-attempt links. Include missed, flagged, post-session skipped, and teacher-selected reasons.\\\\n- Preserve first and optional blind answer, choice state, written explanation, Joshua\'s feedback, and distinct Reveal. Require a concrete error/right-answer diagnosis only for missed-answer completion.\\\\n- Mark reviewed completes the task, not the flag or attempt. A later miss opens a new task.\\\\n## Acceptance\\\\n- [ ] Four source types open the correct content from the queue and return to their filter.\\\\n- [ ] A reviewed flag remains flagged; history and first/blind answers remain unchanged.\\\\n- [ ] A later miss creates a new review episode; teacher selection appears with reason.\\\\n- [ ] Sparse and unsupported-source states never claim a complete queue.\\\\n## Verification\\\\nSource-linked integration samples, direct deep links, keyboard question flow, and teacher/student queue agreement.\\\\n\\\\r\\\\n\\\\n## Audit detail added 2026-09-28\\\\n- Provide a compact default Review queue and an optional advanced question table with reason, first/blind answer, source, time, note, flag, and review state. Filtering and the selected question survive the round trip through a question view.\\\\n- Keep pause-for-review distinct from auto-reveal; the student should know which behavior applies before answering.\\\\r\\\\n\\",\\"number\\":170,\\"state\\":\\"open\\",\\"title\\":\\"Slice 3: unified Review queue and answer workflow\\",\\"updated_at\\":\\"2026-09-28T09:09:37Z\\"}\\n{\\"body\\":\\"Part of implementation spec #165. Decision source: #155. Release order 4/13.\\\\n\\\\n## Outcome\\\\nMy Work provides trustworthy To do, Completed, and Notes \\\\u0026 recaps tabs, shared with Home and the teacher dossier.\\\\n## Build\\\\n- Build one student-scoped work projection from assignment rows, guided drills, classroom items, Activities, bootcamps, and relevant test work. Preserve separate rows for repeat assignments.\\\\n- Derive In progress only from saved work; derive practice Completed only from saved finish/submission. Support attributed Mark done only for open-ended preparation.\\\\n- Keep teacher notes, session and smartboard recaps, and student notes as typed reading records labeled by author; link them to work without counting them as completion.\\\\n## Acceptance\\\\n- [ ] Two assignments of the same guided drill display separately.\\\\n- [ ] Opening a TA/work item does not mark it In progress; saved response does.\\\\n- [ ] An overdue unfinished practice remains To do and may later complete after a saved finish.\\\\n- [ ] Student, Home, and teacher dossier show the same statuses and notes.\\\\n## Verification\\\\nReconcile each state against source rows for representative student accounts; test duplicate issue, saved finish, overdue, and note authorship.\\\\n\\\\r\\\\n\\",\\"number\\":169,\\"state\\":\\"open\\",\\"title\\":\\"Slice 2: My Work ledger, completion, and notes\\",\\"updated_at\\":\\"2026-09-28T09:00:06Z\\"}\\n{\\"body\\":\\"Part of implementation spec #165. Decision source: #155. Release order 3/13.\\\\n\\\\n## Outcome\\\\nStudents navigate Home, My Work, Practice, Review, Progress, Library, and Schedule in one rail with URL-backed page tabs and readable content space.\\\\n## Build\\\\n- Reuse the classroom shell; replace the rail catalog and adapt layouts at phone, laptop, wide, and sidecar widths. Inbox and Find question remain persistent shortcuts.\\\\n- Create destination routes, local tab paths, honest empty states, and lock states. Keep all tabs visible and enforce restrictions on direct URLs.\\\\n- Forward old Journal, flags, analytics, history, work, drills, and exam deep links to the corresponding new content and migrate saved rail preferences once.\\\\n## Acceptance\\\\n- [ ] Every destination and tab opens directly, survives refresh, and copies as a stable link.\\\\n- [ ] Locked student tabs visibly explain access and deny direct routes; admin Admissions remains available.\\\\n- [ ] Old links preserve intended question/work/filter context and saved rail choices migrate once.\\\\n- [ ] Question reading canvas gains space; keyboard and mobile navigation work.\\\\n## Verification\\\\nSix core task walks, direct-route permission tests, and screenshots at narrow, 1024, 1280, 1440, and sidecar-do…68 tokens truncated…## Outcome\\\\nFull PrepTests and standalone timed LR/RC resume safely from account-acknowledged state and stop the clock and progression when cloud saving fails.\\\\n## Build\\\\n- Reconcile from latest main, which already has owner-bound full PrepTest sync, owned Attempts, local backup, and explicit second-device takeover. Verify those features and migrations live; do not rebuild them.\\\\n- Make standalone LR/RC answer, position, and elapsed saves account-acknowledged before progression. A local write alone is not success.\\\\n- On any failed timed save, retain pending device backup, pause clock and next action, show persistent retry state, then resume only after account acknowledgment. Include full PrepTest break/transition clocks.\\\\n- Preserve ownerless browser records untouched and outside account Attempts. Preserve completed results while access gates deny inaccessible resume.\\\\n## Acceptance\\\\n- [ ] Network failure during full PT, LR section, RC section, and break pauses clock/navigation with visible state.\\\\n- [ ] Retry flushes pending work exactly once, then clock resumes without lost or duplicated answers.\\\\n- [ ] Explicit second-device takeover revokes first-device writes; old device cannot overwrite new state.\\\\n- [ ] Access loss preserves run/result but blocks restricted questions; no ownerless run attaches to an account.\\\\n## Verification\\\\nBrowser and backend test with actual network interruption, reload, second device, and access revocation. Confirm target backend migration and RLS, not merely source files.\\\\n\\\\r\\\\n\\",\\"number\\":167,\\"state\\":\\"open\\",\\"title\\":\\"Slice 0B: make every timed exam save failure pause safely\\",\\"updated_at\\":\\"2026-09-28T09:00:02Z\\"}\\n{\\"body\\":\\"Part of implementation spec #165. Decision source: #155. Release order 1/13.\\\\n\\\\n## Outcome\\\\nOne typed student-scoped contract supplies work, attempts, content references, flags, notes, and review eligibility across LR, RC, Activities, bootcamps, assigned work, and exams. The signed-in actor and selected student remain distinct.\\\\n## Build\\\\n- Inventory live source tables, policies, and missing coverage on the target backend. Define stable typed IDs and source adapters, preserving original records and assignment instance IDs.\\\\n- Expose actor/subject scoped reads for student self and authorized teacher/admin. Attribute teacher mutations to the teacher.\\\\n- Add real-data coverage diagnostics so an unsupported source is named, not counted as zero.\\\\n## Acceptance\\\\n- [ ] A student cannot read or mutate another student\'s records via direct request.\\\\n- [ ] Teacher/admin selected-student reads and authorized writes work without impersonation, and record the actor.\\\\n- [ ] LR, RC, Activity, bootcamp, and unstarted assignment samples trace to source rows.\\\\n- [ ] Missing source coverage is disclosed; no silent invented totals.\\\\n## Verification\\\\nDatabase/RLS integration checks and source-to-view reconciliation for representative student accounts. Reuse current migrations where present; confirm live schema before adding another migration.\\\\n\\\\r\\\\n\\",\\"number\\":166,\\"state\\":\\"open\\",\\"title\\":\\"Slice 0A: shared student scope and source coverage contract\\",\\"updated_at\\":\\"2026-09-28T09:00:00Z\\"}\\n{\\"body\\":\\"# LSAT U connected student and teacher experience: implementation spec\\\\n\\\\nDate: 2026-09-28  \\\\nStatus: Ready for implementation planning  \\\\nDecision source: [Wayfinder map #155](https://github.com/vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db/issues/155) and its resolved child tickets  \\\\nDesign source: [platform adoption game plan](2026-09-27-lsatu-platform-adoption-game-plan.md), [student design](2026-09-28-lsatu-student-experience-design.md), and [clickable prototype](prototypes/lsatu-student-experience.html)\\\\n\\\\n## Problem statement\\\\n\\\\nStudents currently find assigned work, recaps, practice, review, analytics, and history through too many rail entries and inconsistent records. Joshua needs the same student state inside his existing teacher workspace, with full teacher and admin controls and without his own practice changing a student\'s results. The redesign should let a student move from an assigned task to an answer, a useful review, and the next action through a short rail and truthful shared data.\\\\n\\\\n## Solution\\\\n\\\\nUse seven stable student destinations: **Home, My Work, Practice, Review, Progress, Library, Schedule**. Keep Inbox and question search as persistent shortcuts. Each destination owns URL-backed local tabs, concise next actions, and clear loading, empty, partial, error, and access states. Keep the teacher desk\'s four rooms, Today, Inbox, Library, Insights, and add a selected-student experience within the existing dossier. Teacher and student views use the same scoped work, attempt, review, and progress rules.\\\\n\\\\nShip student-first, usable vertical slices. Reconcile current `main` before implementation: full PrepTest account sync, owned run lists, and explicit takeover are already coded there, while timed LR/RC save acknowledgment and pause behavior remain incomplete. A local feature checkout is older and must not be used to infer missing features on `main`. Verify migrations and permissions on the target backend before claiming live parity.\\\\n\\\\n## User stories and acceptance\\\\n\\\\n### Navigation and permissions\\\\n\\\\n1. As a student, I see exactly seven main rail destinations, with short labels and one content canvas, so I can locate work without scanning many unrelated links.\\\\n2. As a student, I can open a page or tab by URL and return to the same tab, filters, question, and source context after navigating away.\\\\n3. As a student, I retain access to old links for assignments, Journal, flagged questions, analytics, history, drills, and exams; each forwards to the corresponding new location and item.\\\\n4. As a student, I see every tab even if unavailable, with a lock and explanation; a direct URL enforces the same access restriction.\\\\n5. As a student, I can reach Inbox and Find question from any main page without making either an eighth rail destination.\\\\n6. As a student on a laptop or phone, I can read questions and explanations at a useful width with keyboard and touch controls; the rail never forces a permanently narrow question canvas.\\\\n\\\\n### My Work and the coached loop\\\\n\\\\n7. As a student, My Work has **To do, Completed, Notes \\\\u0026 recaps** tabs with consistent counts and source labels.\\\\n8. As a student, I see every issued assignment, including two separate assignments of the same guided drill, with due date, state, and direct Continue or Open action.\\\\n9. As a student, opening an assignment alone does not mark it In progress; saved answer or response work does.\\\\n10. As a student, practice work becomes Completed only after a saved finish or submission; overdue unfinished work remains To do and can still be finished.\\\\n11. As a student, open-ended preparation may use an attributed Mark done action, while measurable practice cannot be manually completed without its finish record.\\\\n12. As a student, Notes \\\\u0026 recaps shows teacher notes, session and smartboard recaps, and my notes with author and type labels; these reading records do not count as completed practice.\\\\n13. As Joshua, I see the same assignment rows and statuses in the selected student\'s dossier and can act on them using my teacher permissions.\\\\n\\\\n### Practice and exam safety\\\\n\\\\n14. As a student, Practice has **Drills** and **Practice Exams**. Drills offers Smart Drill, saved sets, and a manual builder with exact PT/section/question selection, available count, preview, time, freshness, and clear feedback mode.\\\\n15. As a student, opening a known question lets me choose Practice or Inspect; Inspect does not create a scored attempt or change accuracy.\\\\n16. As a student, answer selection and submission are distinct when the mode requires them; Selected, first answer, blind answer, and correct answer use words as well as color.\\\\n17. As a student, Practice Exams has **Overview, All Exams, Attempts**. Overview provides full PrepTest and timed-section starts and a single unfinished list; All Exams has PrepTest detail; Attempts holds owned in-progress and finished full and timed work with result links.\\\\n18. As a student, I confirm duration, accommodations, source, and access before a timed start; starting is never an accidental side effect of opening a card.\\\\n19. As a student, a new timed run saves answers, position, elapsed time, and result to my account, and I can resume an owned unfinished run on another device after explicitly taking over. The first device then loses its save lease.\\\\n20. As a student, leaving or closing the exam page pauses its clock, while changing tabs with the page still open does not.\\\\n21. As a student, if an account save fails during timed work, a temporary device backup is retained, the next question and clock pause, and a clear save problem remains until an acknowledged retry succeeds. A local write alone does not claim account safety.\\\\n22. As a student whose exam access changes, I retain owned run and result history, but cannot resume a question I can no longer access.\\\\n23. As a student, account Attempts never displays an older browser-only run without a trustworthy owner. Such copies are preserved without automatic claim or import; recovery design is deferred.\\\\n\\\\n### Review and question understanding\\\\n\\\\n24. As a student, Review has **To review, Wrong answers, Flagged** tabs with typed source labels and clear reasons such as missed, flagged, skipped, or from Joshua.\\\\n25. As a student, a Review task points to the source attempt and question even for RC, Activity, or bootcamp content, without assuming an LR bank ID.\\\\n26. As a student, my first answer, optional blind answer, written reasoning, flag, teacher note, and answer-choice explanations remain visible in their correct order; feedback reveal follows the chosen mode.\\\\n27. As a student, before marking a missed question reviewed, I record what made my wrong answer tempting and the exact proof for the right answer. A non-miss flag or skip may be marked reviewed without that required writing.\\\\n28. As a student, Mark reviewed finishes a review task but preserves the independent flag and all attempt history. A later miss creates a new task rather than rewriting the old review.\\\\n29. As Joshua, I can select a question for student review and see its reason and completion without altering the student\'s original answer.\\\\n\\\\n### Progress and Home\\\\n\\\\n30. As a student, Progress has **Overview, History, Topics**, and one 7/14/21/30/All control applies to each visible statistic and history set.\\\\n31. As a student, diagnostic LR and RC accuracy uses only my first saved, graded answer ever to each distinct question, and the timeframe filters that first answer by its graded date. A repeat inside the window cannot become eligible merely because the first answer predates it. Full-test and timed-section answers enter only after saved finish/submission makes them graded. Repeats appear in History and volume; Inspect, blind answers, and ungraded work do not enter accuracy.\\\\n32. As a student, each percentage shows its numerator, eligible-question denominator, source and mode coverage, and any missing sources. LR and RC remain separate. Fewer than three eligible questions shows insufficient evidence; three to nine is labeled an early signal.\\\\n33. As a student, a priority explains its evidence and links to a relevant Review queue or prefilled Practice drill. Assignment attribution appears only when the attempt stores an explicit assignment link.\\\\n34. As Joshua, I see the selected student\'s Progress from the same calculation and scope, with no discrepancy caused by a second analytics formula.\\\\n35. As a student, Home keeps the approved Home v3 structure and shows a reasoned first action, assigned work, conditional message from Joshua, and next seven days; completion updates the entry points without invented counts.\\\\n\\\\n### Library, Schedule, and teacher workspace\\\\n\\\\n36. As a student, Library groups written lessons, Activities, and bootcamps with named reasoning operation, instructions, item count, Continue, finish rule, and direct related practice.\\\\n37. As a student, Schedule links a coming one-to-one session to preparation, then recap and follow-up work, with the same records visible in My Work.\\\\n38. As Joshua, I keep the four existing teacher rooms and the selected-student dossier. In Teacher mode I can view the student\'s pages and use authorized teacher/admin actions with a persistent selected-student label.\\\\n39. As Joshua, my edits to a student\'s work are attributed to me. If I answer a question, the answer belongs to my teacher practice and never affects that student\'s attempts, score, History, completion, Review, or Progress.\\\\n40. As Joshua, Today and the dossier show overdue work, latest attempt, review due, and next session from the same data used on the student\'s pages.\\\\n41. As a student, Admissions stays visible but locked while unfinished; Joshua and admins retain authorized access, including by direct URL. The existing published lock is verified as a prerequisite, not rebuilt.\\\\n42. As a student, Practice Drills groups Create, In progress, Completed, and Saved setups. Continue, Results, and Review appear beside the relevant run; the actual launched question count and RC passage or question unit are clear.\\\\n43. As a student, a finished exam shows a compact result summary and an expandable question table with first and optional blind answer, time, source, notes, flags, and review state.\\\\n44. As a student or Joshua, I can open an advanced Review or History table with useful filters and return to the same filtered question without losing context. The default screen stays concise.\\\\n45. As a student, I can bookmark a question separately from a wrong answer or flag, use relevant passage find, highlighting, and answer elimination tools, and read a second attributed written explanation when Joshua provides one. These tools do not change the original answer.\\\\n46. As a student, I can scan Joshua\'s prescribed week with status, reliable estimated size, and finish rule. His endorsed priority remains visible ahead of automated suggestions; Home explains any recommendation.\\\\n47. As a student, study settings explain the effect of timing accommodations, feedback timing, review rules, analytics inclusion, contrast, and text size before I change them. Future attempts retain mode/source provenance and old results are not silently recalculated.\\\\n\\\\n## Implementation decisions\\\\n\\\\n- Define an explicit actor and subject scope for reads and writes. Student self-service uses the signed-in student; teacher access names a selected student but retains the teacher\'s authenticated identity. Server permissions enforce the scope and attribution.\\\\n- Use typed identifiers for content, assignment instance, attempt, review task, and source. Keep repeated assignment instances distinct. Keep flags, wrong answers, reviews, and notes as related but separate facts.\\\\n- Build a shared student work projection for Home, My Work, and teacher dossier. Build a shared paged attempt projection and pure first-answer reducer for Progress and teacher Insights. Disclose source gaps until all adapters are covered.\\\\n- Use the latest remote `main` as the integration base. Full PrepTest account store and explicit takeover exist in code; verify target backend schema, policies, and published behavior. Repair timed LR/RC cloud acknowledgment and all failed-save clock paths, including any timed break.\\\\n- Preserve ownerless legacy browser records untouched. Do not import, display in account Attempts, or ask students to claim them in this release. Track recovery only as a later evidence-backed question.\\\\n- Keep current curriculum and question engines where they meet the new contract. Adapt source-specific LR, RC, Activity, and bootcamp records into shared views rather than erasing their differences.\\\\n- Gate every destination and local tab at both navigation and direct route. Migrate saved rail preferences once, preserve old deep links, and never grant a locked child page through its parent.\\\\n- Save mode, timing condition, source, and assignment reference when an attempt is created. Preserve first and blind answers separately. Inspect does not create an attempt.\\\\n- Release working routes in slices. Shell and Home design may proceed while data projections are built, but Review and Progress counts cannot be published before source and permission checks pass.\\\\n\\\\n## Testing decisions and release gates\\\\n\\\\n- Use representative student and teacher accounts plus direct database checks to reconcile one unstarted, one started, one completed, and one repeated assignment; a teacher note, a recap, and a student note; LR, RC, Activity, and bootcamp review sources; first and repeat answers.\\\\n- Exercise permissions for student self, another student, Joshua/admin selected-student view, locked tabs, and direct URLs. Confirm teacher-owned practice never mutates a student record.\\\\n- Exercise full PrepTest and standalone timed LR/RC save success, network failure, retry, reload, second-device takeover, access revocation, and finish. Confirm the clock and navigation pause on failed account acknowledgment.\\\\n- Trace every Progress numerator and denominator to eligible attempt IDs for each timeframe; compare the student\'s view with the teacher\'s view. Verify missing coverage and sparse states explicitly.\\\\n- Walk six core tasks: find assignment, find recap, deliberately launch a timed section, revisit a flagged question, inspect without scoring, and open a selected student\'s experience from the teacher desk.\\\\n- Test the real app at narrow phone, ordinary laptop, wide desktop, and sidecar-docked widths. Verify keyboard focus, tab semantics, 44 px touch targets, text zoom, reduced motion, color-independent result labels, and question reading width.\\\\n- End the core rollout with Impeccable critique, audit, fixes, and recheck on all seven student destinations and teacher flows. Resolve all P0/P1 findings and record disposition of lesser findings. This gate follows functional data tests; a prototype screenshot is not evidence of correctness.\\\\n- Each slice must have a usable route, real data or honest empty state, relevant automated checks, and an observed browser task walk before it is marked done.\\\\n\\\\n## Out of scope for this release\\\\n\\\\nNew video production; broad forum, group-class catalog, leaderboards, tutor marketplace, generic streak pressure; outside-result import; capacity-aware auto-planning; a 3D analytics hero; automatic ownership recovery for old browser-only runs; a required current-student prototype test before building; redesigning the teacher desk into new rooms. The viable question tools and study settings are now included in follow-on slices #179 and #180 rather than deferred.\\\\n\\\\n## Further notes\\\\n\\\\n- The Admissions student lock was published on 2026-09-28, but a live admin account was not used for its verification. Recheck admin access during the permission slice.\\\\n- On 2026-09-28, a read of latest `origin/main` found account-backed full PrepTest sync and owned run migration code. The dirty feature checkout lacks it. Neither source inspection nor migrations prove the target backend is deployed; verify live behavior before editing or closing a build ticket.\\\\n- Joshua chose not to wait for a current-student prototype walk. A later student test can inform refinement without blocking this spec.\\\\n- The detailed competitive evidence remains in [the four-platform audit](../research/2026-09-27-lsat-platform-ui-audit.md). This spec adopts patterns suited to LSAT U rather than copying competitors\' visuals or claiming every observed feature is available in LSAT U.\\\\n\\",\\"number\\":165,\\"state\\":\\"open\\",\\"title\\":\\"Spec: connected student and teacher experience\\",\\"updated_at\\":\\"2026-09-28T09:10:28Z\\"}\\n"}'}]

no retroactive score changes. |\\r\\n| Calm card hierarchy, one primary action, restrained color, readable explanation pane | Include as cross-cutting design | [#168](https://github.com/vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db/issues/168), [#170](https://github.com/vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db/issues/170), [#178](https://github.com/vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db/issues/178) | Apply LSAT U tokens, inspect phone/laptop/wide/sidecar, then run Impeccable critique and fixes. |\\r\\n\\r\\n## Ideas we are not building in this release\\r\\n\\r\\n- **New video catalog, group classes, broad forum, tutor marketplace, leaderboards, generic streak pressure:** poor fit for the current one-to-one offer or require ongoing operations Joshua has not chosen.\\r\\n- **LawHub or paper-score import:** ownership, rights, consent, source mapping, and actual demand need a separate decision.
